No requirement states "do not use claims for authorization" or "scope must be looked up server-side". | [ASVS v5.0.0 V9](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/ASVS/v5.0.0/5.0/en/0x18-V9-Self-contained-Tokens.md) (fetched 2026-10-17) | 1 | The "scope looked up per request, not carried in the token" recommendation in § 5 therefore rests on F421/F410 (revocation latency), not on an ASVS line. |
| F412 | OWASP API Security Top 10 2023, **API5:2023 Broken Function Level Authorization**: one consistent authorization module that every business function calls; deny by default, grant each function only to explicitly approved roles; review every endpoint taking the user-group hierarchy into account; administrative controllers inherit a base that enforces role checks. API1 (object level) and API2 IDs are in sync-note F324 and were not re-fetched. | [API5:2023](https://api-security.owasp.org/editions/2023/en/0xa5-broken-function-level-authorization) (fetched 2026-10-17; the `owasp.org` URL answered 308 to this host) | 1 | Owner/manager/cashier is a function-level split (API5); the branch split is object-level (API1, ASVS 8.2.2). |

#### 3.4 Local store isolation: SQLite/SQLCipher, key destruction, media sanitisation, OS copies

| # | Claim | Source | Tier | Notes |
|---|-------|--------|------|-------|
| F413 | SQLite pragma page (version of the page not stated; local library 3.54.0): `secure_delete` is set by a compile-time option and normally off; on, deleted content is overwritten with zeros; FAST zeros only when no extra I/O is needed and "leaves forensic traces on freelist pages"; `VACUUM` afterwards is the other route; only ordinary tables are scrubbed (FTS shadow tables may keep traces). `journal_mode=DELETE` removes the rollback journal at commit; `TRUNCATE` truncates it to zero length; `PERSIST` keeps the file and zeroes only its header (the page does not say what happens to the rest of the contents). `journal_size_limit` truncates a persistent journal or a WAL kept after a checkpoint down to the limit. WAL: by default the `-wal` file is not truncated after a checkpoint; only `TRUNCATE` is described as truncating it, and no mode erases content. `temp_store=MEMORY` keeps temp objects out of files (a compile-time macro can override it). `auto_vacuum=NONE` (default) leaves freed pages on the freelist; `incremental_vacuum` or FULL return them and shorten the file. | [SQLite PRAGMA](https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html) (fetched 2026-10-17; the last 251 characters not read) | 2 | Page says nothing on secure_delete and the WAL. Executed results: F425, F426. |
| F414 | NIST SP 800-88 **Rev. 2** "Guidelines for Media Sanitization", final September 2025, supersedes Rev. 1 (2014-12-17). The page's text does not define cryptographic erase (the term occurs only in its keyword list); the publication PDF was not read for the definition. | [NIST CSRC SP 800-88 Rev. 2](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/88/r2/final) (fetched 2026-10-17) | 1 (abstract page only) | The standard's definition and conditions of crypto-erase: F427 (NOT SOURCEABLE). |
| F415 | ASVS 5.0.0 V14 Data Protection: **14.1.1** (L2) identify and classify sensitive data; **14.1.2** (L2) document protection requirements per level (encryption, integrity, retention, logging); **14.2.4** (L2) implement them; **14.2.7** (L3) retention classification and automatic deletion of outdated data; **14.3.1** (L1) clear authenticated data from client storage and the DOM when the client or session ends; **14.3.2** (L2) anti-caching headers; **14.3.3** (L2) no sensitive data in browser storage (localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB, cookies) except session tokens. V14 has nothing on encrypting a local database on a native client. | [ASVS v5.0.0 V14](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/ASVS/v5.0.0/5.0/en/0x23-V14-Data-Protection.md) (fetched 2026-10-17) | 1 | 14.3.x govern the dashboard SPA (browser), not the Windows cashier store; the cashier store is covered by the key-custody ADR and, by analogy only, 14.1.2/14.2.7. |

The chapter says **nothing on key destruction**, key wrapping, DEK/KEK or vaults. | [ASVS v5.0.0 V11](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/ASVS/v5.0.0/5.0/en/0x20-V11-Cryptography.md) (fetched 2026-10-17) | 1 | Key destruction as a lifecycle step is therefore required only indirectly via 11.1.1 → NIST SP 800-57 (not fetched: NOT SOURCEABLE here). |

Searched: the CSRC abstract page (F414), which does not define it; the publication PDF and NIST SP 800-57 were not fetched (fetch budget). Any "crypto-erase is sufficient" statement in this note is therefore design reasoning `[A]`, not a sourced rule. | [NIST CSRC](https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/88/r2/final) | 1 (page read) | Owner: security-lead (Q-OQ9). |
| F428 | Volume Shadow Copy Service (Microsoft Learn, page updated 2026-02-16): shadow copies are made of whole volumes; the system provider uses copy-on-write, keeping the pre-change blocks in a "diff area" on an NTFS volume, so blocks that existed when a copy was taken stay recoverable from it after the live file changes; `vssadmin delete shadows` removes copies made by the system provider, running out of diff space deletes the oldest copies first; `FilesNotToSnapshot` removes files from copies only on a best-effort basis ("not guaranteed to be deleted"); the page's availability FAQ lists Windows XP to Windows 7 and says nothing about Windows 10/11, and does not say whether System Restore or shadow copies are on by default on a client PC. | [Volume Shadow Copy Service](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/storage/file-server/volume-shadow-copy-service) (fetched 2026-10-17) | 2 | Default state on Windows 10/11 venue PCs, and whether a shadow copy would hold the store file: NOT SOURCEABLE (PENDING - S43, needs the Windows VM, which this session must not touch). |

#### 3.5 Executed evidence (tier E)

Evidence directory: `docs/research/evidence/tenant-isolation/part-4/` (files: `run.sh`, `00-setup.sql`, `00-setup.out`, `10-tests.sql`, `10-tests.out`, `20-mv-rls-try.sql`/`.out`, `versions.out`; `s40b-sqlite-residue.py` run 1 kept as `*.run1-CONFOUNDED.*`, `s40b-note.txt`, `s40b2-secure-delete-off.py`/`.out`). All PostgreSQL rows: `postgres:18.6` (server reported `PostgreSQL 18.6 (Debian 18.6-1.pgdg13+2)`, image sha256:74935e72…c4336), own container `ti-p4-pg` on 127.0.0.1:57441, command `bash run.sh`, 2026-10-16 22:14 UTC by the container clock; the container was removed by the script. Runtime role is a real LOGIN role (`app_rt`, NOSUPERUSER, NOBYPASSRLS, not the owner), connected over TCP, not `SET ROLE`. No pooler, no Go/Kotlin driver. Seed: 2 tenants, tenant A with 3 branches (5,000 bills each), tenant B with 1; 10 + 4 catalogue items. In the output file a few `ERROR:` lines appear after the following `BEGIN` because stderr and stdout are interleaved by buffering; the statement they belong to is the one named in the preceding `\echo`.

| F517 | **PostgreSQL 18 per-role defaults.** `ALTER ROLE ... SET parameter` sets a role's default for **new sessions only** ("only happens at login time"); `SET ROLE` does not apply it; a role-and-database setting overrides role-only, which overrides database-only; `ALTER ROLE ... CONNECTION LIMIT n` is a per-role attribute (the ALTER ROLE page defers its meaning to CREATE ROLE, not fetched here). Consequence for the recommended shared-schema-RLS model (part-1): the runtime role is **shared by all tenants**, so a role-level `statement_timeout` or connection limit cannot differ per tenant; a per-tenant or per-plan value must be applied per transaction (`SET LOCAL`) next to the tenant context, or by role-per-plan-tier. The role-per-tenant variant exists only under schema/db-per-tenant. | [PG 18 ALTER ROLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-alterrole.html) | 2 | "Consequence" is inference, not a quoted statement. `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` behind PgBouncer transaction mode is the same mechanism as `set_config(..., true)` (part-1 F115) but was not run for this parameter: PENDING — S50 if wanted. |
| F518 | **PostgreSQL 18 timeouts.** `statement_timeout` (default 0 = off, ms) aborts a statement over the limit, applied per statement; `lock_timeout` (default off) limits a wait for one lock; `transaction_timeout` (default off) limits a whole transaction, explicit or implicit, not prepared transactions; `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` (default off) ends a session idle inside a transaction (such sessions hold locks and block vacuum). The docs advise **against** setting `statement_timeout` in `postgresql.conf` because it hits every session; set per role, database or session. If `transaction_timeout` <= another timeout, the longer one has no effect. | [PG 18 runtime-config-client](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/runtime-config-client.html) | 2 | All four are off by default, so without explicit config a noisy tenant's query is bounded by nothing; the part-2 finding F214 (leaked open transaction starves a pool) is the pool-side partner of `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`. |

| F520 | **Traefik 3.7 `RateLimit` middleware.** Token bucket: `average` per `period` (default 1 s) refills it, `burst` is its capacity (default 1); `average` default 0 disables it. `sourceCriterion` picks the key: remote address by default, or `requestHost`, `requestHeaderName` (any header) or `ipStrategy` (X-Forwarded-For depth, excluded proxy IPs, IPv6 subnet). Without a `redis` block counters are **in memory per Traefik instance**; with `redis` configured they are shared. The page does **not** state the response status or headers on rejection: NOT SOURCEABLE from this page (no 429, `Retry-After` or `RateLimit` header claim can be made for Traefik). A header-keyed limit is only as trustworthy as the header: part-2 says the backend must never take a tenant from a header (part-2 X3); a tenant/device identity that exists only after signature verification (part-3 D3-A) is not visible to the proxy. | [Traefik v3.7 RateLimit](https://doc.traefik.io/traefik/v3.7/reference/routing-configuration/http/middlewares/ratelimit/) | 2 | Pinned Traefik is 3.7.14; the docs page is for v3.7. |
| F521 | **pgBackRest (a backup tool the stack reference lists; not pinned).** Encryption is per repository: `repo-cipher-type` (`none` default, `aes-256-cbc`) with a `repo-cipher-pass` passphrase, applied client-side; the page does not list which file types are covered. Retention is by count or time of full backups (`repo-retention-full`, `-type`), archived WAL older than the oldest retained full backup expires automatically unless archive retention is set, and expiring a full backup expires its dependent differentials and incrementals. One key and one retention for the whole cluster backup: **a backup-tool key cannot be per tenant**, and one tenant's data cannot be removed from a backup without expiring that backup. Stack reference lists pgBackRest 2.59.3 (2026-10-04, repo archived 2026-04-27 then revived), Barman 3.20.1, WAL-G; the tool is undecided. | [pgBackRest configuration](https://pgbackrest.org/configuration.html); `docs/stack-reference/postgres/best-practices.md` § 4 | 2 (vendor docs) / 2 (stack ref) | Tool choice is `data-lead`/`sre-lead`; this finding holds for any physical (base backup + WAL) tool since PG continuous archiving restores the whole cluster (part-1 F118). |
| F522 | **Comparison only (UK GDPR regulator, not Egyptian law).** The ICO says an erasure request reaches backups as well as live systems, but backups may keep the data until overwritten provided it is put "beyond use" (no other use, kept only until replaced on an established schedule) and the individual is told what happens in backups; erasure does not apply where the law requires the data to be kept (example: employer salary records for the tax authority); response within one month, extendable by two. | [ICO, right to erasure](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/individual-rights/individual-rights/right-to-erasure/) | 3 (regulator guidance, foreign) | The ICO page says it is under review. Egypt's regulation has no equivalent "beyond use" text in what was read (F507, F508): whether the PDPC accepts it is **needs counsel**. |
| F523 | **Comparison only (EU GDPR text).** Art. 17(3) lets erasure be refused where retention is necessary for a legal obligation under EU or Member State law or for legal claims; Art. 17(1) lists the erasure grounds. | [GDPR Art. 17 (gdpr-info.eu, unofficial copy of the text)](https://gdpr-info.eu/art-17-gdpr/) | 1 (text, foreign, unofficial host) | The Egyptian law's analogue is the "legitimate reason to retain" wording in Art. 4 (F507); Egypt's text does not list exceptions in the same form. |
| F524 | **GCRA** keeps one stored value per key, the "theoretical arrival time" (TAT); a request is allowed if `now` is after `TAT - burst buffer`, and TAT then advances by the emission interval; compared with time-bucketed counters it spreads load instead of allowing a quota to be used at the start of a window. Clock handling: use the store's time (Redis `TIME`) so instances' clocks do not cause false rejections. One article, one author. | [Brandur, rate limiting](https://brandur.org/rate-limiting) | 4 (corroborate) | The article does not cover token bucket or Lua; it is a lead for the algorithm choice, not the basis. A normative GCRA definition (ITU/ATM Forum): NOT SOURCEABLE (not fetched). |
| F525 | **Product constraints from internal notes.** (a) Sync-note OQ29 (founder rule 2026-10-16, provisional): the device keeps business records 60 days; the server backup/PITR window must be shorter than that (the exact figure is `data-lead`/`sre-lead`'s proposal, not set); outbox rows are deleted only after a fully parsed ack. (b) Brief item 11 / line 48: a tenant is active or suspended; the cashier app has a bounded, known offline grace after which it must reach the server to keep selling; the grace length is open (`/license-design`). (c) eta-note D5 (founder condition): the archive of tax records lives on the hosted server only, at least five years, per-tenant configurable, with owner export carrying a hash manifest. | `docs/research/local-first-sync.md` OQ29; `design/product-brief.md` lines 48, 73; `docs/research/eta-e-receipt-e-invoice.md` D5, R3 | internal | Not re-researched. Numbers other than 60 days and 5 years are not set anywhere. |

The chapter has **no backup-specific requirement** (it falls under 14.1.2/14.2.4 only if the documented protection levels cover backups). V2 Validation and Business Logic: **2.4.1** (L2) anti-automation against excessive calls (exfiltration, quota exhaustion, DoS, costly resources); **2.1.3** (L2) document business-logic limits per user and across the application; **2.3.2** (L2) implement those limits as documented; **2.4.2** (L3) human-realistic timing. | [ASVS v5.0.0 V14](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/ASVS/v5.0.0/5.0/en/0x23-V14-Data-Protection.md), [V2](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/ASVS/v5.0.0/5.0/en/0x11-V2-Validation-and-Business-Logic.md) | 1 | Tenant-isolation IDs (8.4.1, 8.2.2, 13.2.2) are in part-1 F125. | F528 | **OWASP API Security Top 10 2023, API4:2023 Unrestricted Resource Consumption.** Missing or wrong limits on execution time, memory, upload size, operations per request, records per page or third-party spend expose an API; attackers use many concurrent requests, large result sets, or **batching many operations in one request**, which defeats a per-HTTP-request limit (the page's GraphQL example). Prevention list: container caps on memory/CPU/file descriptors, maximum sizes for inputs and arrays, rate limits per client over time windows tuned to business need (stricter on sensitive operations), server-side validation of page-size parameters, spending limits on third-party services. A sync endpoint takes batches by design, so a limit counted only in requests misses the cost: count commands and bytes too. | [OWASP API4:2023](https://api-security.owasp.org/editions/2023/en/0xa4-unrestricted-resource-consumption) | 1 | The first URL (`owasp.org/API-Security/...`) redirected (308) to this host; fetched at the new host. |

#### 3.3 OWASP API Security Top 10 2023 (tier 1)

| # | Claim | Source (link) | Tier | Notes |
|---|-------|---------------|------|-------|
| F610 | Titles confirmed from the edition index: **API1:2023 Broken Object Level Authorization; API2 Broken Authentication; API3 Broken Object Property Level Authorization; API4 Unrestricted Resource Consumption; API5 Broken Function Level Authorization; API6 Unrestricted Access to Sensitive Business Flows; API7 SSRF; API8 Security Misconfiguration; API9 Improper Inventory Management; API10 Unsafe Consumption of APIs.** API1 page: every endpoint that takes an object id and acts on it needs an object-level check; comparing the session's user id with the id parameter is not a sufficient fix; the prevention list asks for tests of the authorization mechanism and says not to deploy changes that make them fail. API5 page: asks whether a group-X user can reach a group-Y function by guessing the URL (its example path is `export_all`) or by changing the HTTP method. API3 page: covers both excessive data exposure and mass assignment. | [index](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/API-Security/master/editions/2023/en/0x11-t10.md), [API1](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/API-Security/master/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization.md), [API5](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/API-Security/master/editions/2023/en/0xa5-broken-function-level-authorization.md), [API3](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OWASP/API-Security/master/editions/2023/en/0xa3-broken-object-property-level-authorization.md) | 1 | Agrees with sync-note F324; resolves part-2 F291 "API Top 10 BOLA: NOT SOURCEABLE". The API2, API6, API8, API9 pages were saved but only their index summaries are relied on here. |

#### 3.4 Platform documentation for the Android tests (tier 2; fetched 2026-10-17)

| # | Claim | Source (link) | Tier | Notes |
|---|-------|---------------|------|-------|
| F611 | **Auto Backup.** For apps targeting API 23+, backup includes shared preferences, internal-storage files, databases and app-specific external files by default and always excludes cache, code-cache and no-backup directories. `android:allowBackup` defaults to true. From Android 12 (targeting API 31+) `android:dataExtractionRules` has separate `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>` sections and a missing section means that transfer type is fully enabled; `android:fullBackupContent` is for Android 11 and lower, so an app targeting 31+ still needs it for older devices. `<exclude>` beats `<include>`; paths take no wildcards. On Android 12+ some OEMs still allow device-to-device transfer when `allowBackup` is false. The page advises against keeping credentials or tokens in shared preferences or files and excluding device-specific identifiers such as FCM tokens. | [Auto Backup](https://developer.android.com/identity/data/autobackup) | 2 | Page undated; our targetSdk is 36 (compose/VERSION.md). Fills the gap `compose/best-practices.md` line 45 left NOT SOURCEABLE. |
| F612 | **Testing backup/restore.** Scripted `adb` flows: `bmgr enable true`, select the local transport, `bmgr backupnow <package>`, save APKs, uninstall, reinstall; a similar device-to-device flow with a D2D transport and a test-mode setting; `bmgr run` triggers a key-value backup first when needed. No procedure to check that excluded files are NOT restored; the nearest statement is that the cache directory is not backed up. | [Test backup and restore](https://developer.android.com/identity/data/testingbackup) | 2 | Listing the app's data paths after restore is our addition; reading private paths needs a debuggable build (that remark is from the fetch summariser, a lead, not page text). |
| F613 | **App Links.** Verification needs `android:autoVerify="true"` on a VIEW/BROWSABLE/DEFAULT http(s) intent filter and a Digital Asset Links file at `/.well-known/assetlinks.json` on each host; a domain that does not reach `verified` is not handled automatically (the user can still choose the app manually); on Android 15+ changes to the file can take up to seven days; inspection via `adb shell pm get-app-links` and `pm verify-app-links --re-verify`. The page says nothing about validating data inside an incoming link. | [Verify App Links](https://developer.android.com/training/app-links/verify-android-applinks) | 2 | NOT SOURCEABLE: Android's guidance for validating deep-link parameters (the page links to pages not fetched). MASTG-TEST-0394 is used as the checklist. |

#### 3.7 Executed evidence: database-level tests (tier E; spike S60)

Files: `docs/research/evidence/tenant-isolation/part-6/s60/` (`setup.sql`, `catalogue.sql`, `runtime.sql`, `runtime-r4.sql`, `owner.sql`, `run.sh`; outputs `output.run1.txt`, `output.run2.txt`, `output.txt`). Command `bash s60/run.sh`; container `ti-p6-pg` (`postgres:18.6`, no published port, removed by the script; `docker ps -a --filter name=ti-p6` returned nothing afterwards); 2026-10-17. Probe schema, not product code: five tables (one correct, one ENABLE-without-FORCE, one without RLS, one forced with no policy, one with a tenant-and-branch policy), two views, roles `kodeebo_owner` and `kodeebo_app` (non-superuser, `NOBYPASSRLS`; the runtime role owns nothing).

| # | Claim | Source | Tier | Notes |
|---|-------|--------|------|-------|
| F617 | **Catalogue queries find the planted defects.** C1 over `pg_class`/`pg_policy` for every table with a `tenant_id` column: `orders`, `cash` enabled+forced+1 policy = ok; `items` enabled but not forced = `FAIL: not FORCE`; `notes` = `FAIL: RLS not enabled`; `locked` forced with 0 policies = `WARN: no policy (deny all)`. C2: `kodeebo_app` and `kodeebo_owner` not superuser, not bypassrls; `postgres` both; runtime role owns no table. C3: view `items_report` (no options) flagged, `items_report_safe` (`security_invoker=true`) ok. C5 (third run): every unique index whose first column is not `tenant_id` flagged (all five `*_pkey`, as designed). | `s60/catalogue.sql`, `s60/output.txt` | E | C4 (SECURITY DEFINER without pinned `search_path`) returned 0 rows because the probe has no such function: query shape only. Materialised views, partitioned tables, FKs: PENDING — S62. |
| F618 | **Runtime role fails closed without tenant context.** No `app.tenant_id`: 0 rows in `orders`, `items`, `cash`. Setting forced to empty string in a transaction: 0 rows, no error. After `SET LOCAL app.tenant_id` in a COMMITTED transaction the setting reads back as an empty string and `orders` again gives 0 rows. INSERT with no context fails the RLS check. Policy form used: `tenant_id = nullif(current_setting('app.tenant_id', true), '')::uuid`. | `s60/runtime.sql` R1-R3, `s60/output.txt` | E | Agrees with part-3 F324 and restaurant-menu B25/B26. A policy using a plain cast of the empty string may error instead of returning 0 rows: PENDING — S63. A pooled-connection test through pgx/Hikari: PENDING — S61. |
| F619 | **Cross-tenant forms under tenant A's context** (`runtime-r4.sql`, one transaction per statement): read of B's row by id = 0 rows; INSERT carrying B's `tenant_id` fails the RLS check; UPDATE of B's row = `UPDATE 0`; DELETE of B's row = `DELETE 0`; UPDATE of A's row to B's `tenant_id` fails the RLS check; INSERT reusing B's primary-key value fails with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "orders_pkey"`. | `s60/runtime-r4.sql`, `s60/output.txt` | E | The last result is the **id-existence oracle** (sync-note F128 shape) on a key that does not lead with `tenant_id`; the error names the constraint. **Wrong test, kept:** R4 inside `runtime.sql` (`output.run1.txt`) ran all statements in one transaction; it aborted at the first error, so UPDATE and DELETE were never evaluated. It proves nothing for them; `runtime-r4.sql` (`output.run2.txt`, `output.txt`) replaces it. |
| F620 | **Branch scope as a second policy column.** Tenant A + branch 1: only the branch-1 `cash` row visible; tenant A without branch: 0 rows; inserting a branch-2 row under branch-1 context fails the RLS check. A forced table with no policy returns 0 rows even with the right tenant. | `s60/runtime.sql` R5-R6 | E | Shows fail-closed outcomes, not how branch scope should be enforced (other part). |
| F621 | **What a missing control costs.** Table without RLS: tenant A's context reads B's note. View without `security_invoker`: with A's context the aggregate view returned both tenants' counts, a direct SELECT returned 1 row, the `security_invoker` view only A. Table owner without FORCE: owner session with no context sees 2 `items` rows but 0 `orders` rows. Runtime role: `SET row_security = off` made the query error, `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` failed ("must be owner"), `SET ROLE kodeebo_owner` denied. | `s60/runtime.sql` R7-R9, `s60/owner.sql`, `s60/output.txt` | E | The view leak is the database form of the "aggregated reports" case (DA-05, DB-04). `security_invoker` is shown by execution, not documented here: the PG 18 view documentation was not fetched (NOT SOURCEABLE — fetch priorities). |

#### 3.8 Reused findings (not re-researched; tier as stated in the source note)

| # | Claim | Source | Tier | Notes |
|---|-------|--------|------|-------|
| F622 | Sync inbox on 18.6: replay returns `duplicate:applied`; same command id with a different payload is flagged; a tenant-leading key `(tenant_id, device_id, command_id)` lets two tenants use the same command id without conflict, whereas a key on `command_id` alone reveals that another tenant used it; an insert carrying another tenant's id, or `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` aimed at another tenant's row, fails the policy and leaves the row unchanged. | sync-note F124, F128 | E | Basis of SY-05, SY-06, DB-06. |
| F623 | Device binding outcomes to verify from outside (recommended in part-3 §5, pending user decision): ids come from the credential (D3-A), a differing id is refused with an event under the credential's tenant; composite FK `(tenant_id, device_id, branch_id)` rejects cross-branch and cross-tenant inbox rows; revoke versus in-flight command needs `FOR SHARE` on the device row; a restore resurrects a revoked device unless the journal is replayed; a refusal with no matching credential has no tenant and goes to a platform sink. | part-3 F322-F326, F329, F330 | E | Basis of SY-01..SY-04, SY-07, SY-08, SY-15. |
| F624 | The sibling product's pattern for owner-API isolation tests: M2 (A's session plus `X-Tenant-Id: <B>` and B's id in body/query returns only A's data), M1/M3 (spoofed intermediary headers), M11 (log-injection characters in a path id), M13 (log grep), with an ASVS table in its §8. A pattern, not a Kodeebo requirement. | restaurant-menu threat model §6, §8 (read only) | internal | Its ASVS IDs were re-checked against the same v5.0.0 files (F604). |

#### 3.10 The negative-test catalogue (derived from F601-F624; a proposal, tier "derived", not a finding)

Conventions: A and B are two tenants; A1, A2 two branches of A; "A-owner", "A-mgr-1" (manager assigned to A1), "A-cashier-1" are principals; "dev-A1" is the enrolled device of A1. Tool columns: **Go** = Go `testing` + testcontainers-go (unpinned, part-2); **Ktor** = `testApplication` + Testcontainers Java (unpinned); **Web** = Playwright 1.64.0 `request`/browser; **Droid** = Compose `ui-test-junit4` + `adb` scripts. "Contract" = a test run against `contracts/` OpenAPI (tool not chosen, F615). Expected "refused" means a non-2xx with an RFC 9457 problem body that does not echo another tenant's data or constraint names (ASVS 16.5.1). Whether a foreign object answers 404 or 403 is open (Q3); tests assert "same response as a non-existent id" until decided.

**Sync endpoint (SY)** — layer: integration (HTTP against the real server + Testcontainers Postgres) unless stated.

| ID | Preconditions | Action | Expected result | Layer / tool | Evidences |
|----|---------------|--------|-----------------|--------------|-----------|
| SY-01 | dev-A1 enrolled; B exists | Valid signed request whose payload carries tenant B's `tenant_id` | Refused; no inbox row in A or B; security event recorded under A with credential-derived ids; owner alert raised (founder OQ6) | integration; Go / Ktor | ASVS 8.4.1, 8.3.1, 16.3.3; API1 |
| SY-02 | dev-A1; branch A2 and a branch B1 exist | Payload names branch A2, then B1, as `branch_id` | Both refused; no row; event; the composite-key backstop is also exercised at DB level (DB-08) | integration | ASVS 8.2.2, 8.4.1; API1 |
| SY-03 | dev-A1; dev-B1 exists | Payload names dev-B1's `device_id`; separately names a `device_id` of another A device | Refused with event; nothing written to dev-B1's or the other device's sequence | integration | ASVS 8.2.2, 15.3.3; API1, API3 |
| SY-04 | none | (a) No credential; (b) unknown key id; (c) well-formed but altered body; (d) expired/stale signature (rule per part-3 F302) | Refused; response body and status identical across (a)-(c) for any attacker-visible field; event goes to the platform sink (no tenant), no tenant row written; no owner alert | integration | ASVS 16.3.1, 16.5.1; API2 |
| SY-05 | B has command id X applied | dev-A1 sends command id X (own seq) | Treated as A's new command (tenant-leading key); response identical to a fresh id; B's row unchanged | integration | ASVS 8.4.1; API1 |
| SY-06 | B holds `(device, seq)` and command ids known to the attacker | dev-A1 sends ids and sequence numbers likely used in B; compare responses, bodies, timing class | No observable difference from unused ids; no constraint name, no "already exists" for B's data | integration + response snapshot | ASVS 16.5.1, 8.4.1; API1 |
| SY-07 | dev-A1 revoked | Send a command with the revoked credential; send one mid-flight while the revoke runs | Refused `device_not_active` with a security event; the in-flight command is ordered either before or after the revoke, never applied after it committed | integration, two concurrent sessions | ASVS 8.3.2, 15.4.2, 16.3.3 |
| SY-08 | dev-A1 replaced; new device row/credential for the same PC | Old credential and old `device_id` send commands; new device reuses old `device_seq` values | Old refused; new device has its own sequence; no collision with old rows | integration | ASVS 6.4.4 (via part-3 F309), 8.3.2 |
| SY-09 | dev-A1 and dev-B1 feeds contain different data | dev-A1 pulls with B's cursor value, with a forged/truncated cursor, with a cursor from another branch of A | Refused or empty; never any B or other-branch row; cursor is not accepted as an id source | integration | ASVS 8.2.2, 8.4.1; API1 |
| SY-10 | feed endpoint exists | dev-A1 pulls with query/body `tenant_id`, `branch_id`, `device_id` of others | Parameters ignored or refused; result equals the plain pull | contract + integration | ASVS 15.3.3, 15.3.7 |
| SY-11 | A1 and A2 each have a device | dev-A1 pulls/pushes facts belonging to A2 (ids guessed) | Refused or empty (branch scope) | integration | ASVS 8.2.2; API1 |
| SY-12 | OQ6 rule | (a) forged-id command; (b) genuine counter money fact with a review reason | (a) refused + event + alert; (b) **applied and queued**, never rejected | integration | founder rule OQ6, ASVS 16.3.3 (tests the rule's both halves) |
| SY-13 | contract schema | Extra fields (`tenant_id`, `role`, `status`) in body; duplicated parameters; oversized ids | Rejected or ignored; no effect on stored ids | contract + unit | ASVS 15.3.3, 15.3.7, 2.2.1 |
| SY-14 | DB level | `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` aimed at B's row from A's context | Policy error; B's row unchanged | integration (SQL) | ASVS 8.4.1 (F622) |
| SY-15 | concurrent | Run SY-07 with and without the `FOR SHARE` read to prove the test can fail | Test fails against the lock-free variant (guards against a vacuous test) | integration | ASVS 15.4.2 (part-3 F329) |
| SY-16 | log capture | Claimed ids containing CR/LF, quotes, very long strings | Log lines remain valid single JSON records; ids truncated/escaped; no secret or key material | integration + log parse | ASVS 16.4.1, 16.2.5 |

**Owner dashboard API (DA)** — layers: contract (schema matrix from `contracts/`), integration (real server + Postgres), Playwright (SPA).

| ID | Preconditions | Action | Expected result | Layer / tool | Evidences |
|----|---------------|--------|-----------------|--------------|-----------|
| DA-01 | one object of every type in B (and branch A2) | A-owner requests each object type by B's id on every read route (generated from the contract's path-parameter list) | Same response as a non-existent id; generation fails the build when a new route with an id parameter has no case | contract + integration; Web `request` for the HTTP leg | ASVS 8.2.2, 8.4.1; API1 |
| DA-02 | as DA-01 | PUT/PATCH/DELETE and every state-changing action on B's ids | Same refusal; B's object unchanged (verified by direct read) | integration | ASVS 8.2.2, 8.4.1; API1 |
| DA-03 | none | A-owner calls with `X-Tenant-Id`-style headers, `?tenant_id=`, body `tenant_id`, JWT/session claims edited | Ignored/refused; results are A's only | integration; Web `request` | ASVS 4.1.3, 15.3.3, 7.2.1; API1, API3 |
| DA-04 | B and A each have > 1 page of rows | List endpoints: pages, cursors from B, filters, sorting, `limit` beyond max, empty filters | Only A rows; totals, counts and facet values contain only A; B cursor refused | integration | ASVS 8.4.1, 15.3.1; API1, API3 |
| DA-05 | reports exist for both tenants | Aggregated reports, dashboards, materialised summaries, comparisons across branches | Figures equal A's own data computed independently; DB views fixed per DB-04 | integration (golden totals) | ASVS 8.4.1; API1 |
| DA-06 | export feature | (a) A exports; (b) B's user requests A's export job id/URL; (c) manager exports their branch; (d) token-less URL guessing | (a) rows only A (and only permitted branches); (b),(d) refused; (c) only the manager's branch; job runs with the requester's scope, not a service identity; CSV formula cells neutralised | integration + file inspection | ASVS 8.3.3, 8.2.2, 1.2.10 (L3); API1, API5 |
| DA-07 | search/suggest endpoints | Search for strings only present in B, prefix suggest, typo-tolerant search | Zero B hits, zero B counts, no timing-dependent difference by design | integration | ASVS 8.4.1; API1 |
| DA-08 | A-mgr-1 assigned to A1 | Read/write A2 objects by id, list, report, export; owner endpoints | A2 refused/empty; A1 allowed; A-owner sees both | integration; Web | ASVS 8.2.2, 8.2.1; API1, API5 |
| DA-09 | A-mgr-1 | Reassign the manager from A1 to A2, then request A1 data with the still-valid session | Access changes immediately (no stale session claim) | integration | ASVS 8.3.2 |
| DA-10 | roles owner/manager/cashier | Lower role calls higher-role endpoints; swap HTTP method; guess admin paths | Refused | contract + integration | ASVS 8.2.1; API5 |
| DA-11 | sensitive fields exist (staff passcode, cost) | Manager reads fields beyond role; writes `role`, `tenant_id`, `branch_id`, `is_owner` in body | Fields absent / write ignored or refused | contract + integration | ASVS 8.2.3, 15.3.1, 15.3.3; API3 |
| DA-12 | session exists | Use the token after logout; after disabling the user; after tenant suspension | Refused | integration | ASVS 7.4.1, 7.4.2 |
| DA-13 | cookie-session variant chosen (mechanism unset) | Cross-origin calls from a foreign origin, state change by GET, preflight bypass | Allow-list only; unsafe methods only | Web (Playwright) | ASVS 3.4.2, 3.5.1-3.5.3, 3.3.x |
| DA-14 | none | Compare 404/403/409 bodies for existing-foreign vs non-existent ids; unique-name collisions across tenants | Identical; uniqueness is per tenant (a name used in B is free in A with no hint) | integration | ASVS 16.5.1, 8.4.1; API1 |
| DA-15 | any response cache/CDN in use | A then B request the same URL; check `Cache-Control` | No shared-cache hit across tenants; private/no-store on authenticated data | integration + header check | ASVS 14.2.2, 14.3.2; API8 |
| DA-16 | Playwright, two accounts of different tenants | Log in A, browse, log out, log in B in same browser profile; reload; open back-navigation; inspect storage | No A data in DOM, query cache, localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB; back button shows login | Web (Playwright 1.64.0) | ASVS 14.3.1, 14.3.3 |
| DA-17 | sync data exists | Owner of A opens device events, review queue, offline periods; B's owner and A-mgr-2 try the same | Only the owning tenant (and permitted branch) sees them | integration | ASVS 8.4.1, 8.2.2 |
| DA-18 | background jobs | Trigger job/worker with no tenant context, and with a deleted tenant | Fails closed (no rows, error logged), never "all tenants" | integration | ASVS 16.5.3, 8.4.1 |

**Owner Android app (AN)** — the server-side tests above are the isolation proof; these tests cover what is new on the device.

| ID | Preconditions | Action | Expected result | Layer / tool | Evidences |
|----|---------------|--------|-----------------|--------------|-----------|
| AN-01 | contract exists | Run the DA-01..DA-12 matrix with the app's actual HTTP client/config (recorded requests); inspect that requests carry no tenant id field | Same results as the web client; no client-supplied tenant/branch id | contract; Droid (client wiring), tool for server leg as DA | MASVS-AUTH-1; ASVS 8.3.1; API1 |
| AN-02 | account A logged in, data loaded | Logout; inspect app private storage (databases, prefs, files, caches, in-memory singletons after process restart) | No A data or A token remains; the next launch shows login | Droid instrumented + `adb` file listing (debug build) | MASVS-STORAGE-1/2; MASTG-TEST-0207/0287 as procedure (F609); ASVS 14.3.1 by analogy |
| AN-03 | A and B can both log in | Switch A→B without killing the process, then rotate the device, background/foreground, navigate back | No A screen, list item, cached image, ViewModel state or Flow replay shows | Droid (`createAndroidComposeRule`, F615) | MASVS-STORAGE-2; ASVS 14.3.1 by analogy |
| AN-04 | A logged in; owner disables the user server-side | App makes a request | 401/403, app clears state and returns to login | Droid + test server | ASVS 7.4.2; MASVS-AUTH-1 |
| AN-05 | release build | Static: `allowBackup`, `dataExtractionRules`, `fullBackupContent` exclude databases, prefs, files holding tenant data and tokens; dynamic: `bmgr` cloud and D2D flows, then inspect restored data | Nothing tenant-bound restored; after restore the app requires login | static manifest check in CI; Droid + `adb` scripts per F612 | MASVS-STORAGE-2; MASTG-TEST-0262 (static), MASTG-TEST-0216 (dynamic); F611 |
| AN-06 | logged in as A | Open a link `kodeebo://…` / https App Link carrying an id of B's object, an id of A2 as A-mgr-1, a malformed id, an unknown host | App sends the id to the API with A's token; shows the same "not found" as for a missing id; never auto-switches account; malformed input rejected before use; only verified hosts open the app | Droid + `adb shell am start` ; manifest check | MASVS-PLATFORM-1, MASVS-CODE-4; MASTG-TEST-0393, MASTG-TEST-0394; F613 |
| AN-07 | notifications used | Notification for A arrives while B is active; notification text contents | Not shown (or generic text) after account change; no cross-account tap target | Droid | MASVS-PLATFORM-3; MASTG-TEST-0315 |
| AN-08 | debug and release builds | Capture logcat during login/list/export | No tokens, tenant ids, PII | Droid + logcat parse | MASVS-STORAGE-2; MASTG-TEST-0203/0231; ASVS 16.2.5 |
| AN-09 | manifest | Exported activities/services/receivers not requiring permission that can open data screens | None exported without need; those exported do nothing without a session | static manifest check | MASVS-PLATFORM-1; MASTG-TEST-0364/0365/0366 |
| AN-10 | none | Token storage: not in plain prefs or files; excluded from backup | Held with platform keystore (replacement for the deprecated EncryptedSharedPreferences: NOT SOURCEABLE in the stack reference) | static + Droid | MASVS-STORAGE-1, MASVS-CRYPTO-2 |

**Database level (DB)** — integration on `postgres:18.6` via Testcontainers; the SQL is `evidence/.../s60/`. If the cto picks schema- or database-per-tenant, DB-01..DB-04 change to "every tenant schema/database has the same objects and grants" and DB-07..DB-09 become connection-routing tests.

| ID | Preconditions | Action | Expected result | Layer / tool | Evidences |
|----|---------------|--------|-----------------|--------------|-----------|
| DB-01 | all migrations applied | Run catalogue C1 over every table with `tenant_id`; list tables WITHOUT `tenant_id` and compare with an explicit allow-list of global tables | Zero rows with RLS off, not FORCE, or no policy; unlisted tenant-less tables fail the build (second query is a design idea, not run) | integration; Go / Ktor | ASVS 8.4.1, 8.3.1; F617 |
| DB-02 | roles created | C2: runtime, job, migration roles not superuser, not `BYPASSRLS`; runtime role owns no table | Pass | integration | ASVS 13.2.2; F617 |
| DB-03 | none | Runtime role: `SET row_security = off`, DISABLE RLS, `SET ROLE` owner | All fail | integration | ASVS 13.2.2; F621 |
| DB-04 | views exist | C3: every view over tenant tables has `security_invoker=true` (or is justified); aggregate view read under A's context | Only A's figures | integration | ASVS 8.4.1; F617, F621 |
| DB-05 | functions exist | C4: every SECURITY DEFINER function has a pinned `search_path`; `EXECUTE` not public | Zero violations | integration | ASVS 13.2.2; part-3 F327 (query shape only, F617) |
| DB-06 | schema | C5: every unique index/PK on a tenant table leads with `tenant_id` (or is reviewed) | Zero violations; otherwise an id-existence oracle (sync-note F128) | integration | ASVS 8.4.1; F619, F622 |
| DB-07 | runtime role connection | Query every tenant table with no tenant setting, with an empty one, and after a committed `SET LOCAL` | 0 rows, no error; write fails | integration | ASVS 16.5.3, 8.4.1; F618 |
| DB-08 | A's context | Read/insert/update/delete B rows, move own row to B, reuse B key; wrong branch | Read 0, writes `0` rows or policy error | integration | ASVS 8.2.2, 8.4.1; F619, F620 |
| DB-09 | pooled connections | Two requests (A then B) on one pooled connection; B's request with context lost | B never sees A's context or rows | integration through the real pool (pgx 5.11.0 / Hikari 7.1.0) | ASVS 8.4.1; PENDING — S61 |
| DB-10 | migration | A new table in a story migration without RLS | DB-01 fails the pipeline | integration/CI | ASVS 8.4.1 |

## 4. Options Considered

### 4.1 Decision areas across the six parts

| # | Area | Part | Options | Recommended (pending user decision) | Rests on | Decides |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Tenancy model | 1 | A shared-schema-rls · B schema-per-tenant · C db-per-tenant · D A plus a tenant-to-connection resolver port so one tenant can move to its own database | A built so D stays possible | tier 2 + tier E at 18.6 (F101-F123); S13, S14 PENDING only for B/C | `cto` (ADR); counsel on physical isolation / data location |
| 2 | Tenant context in the transaction, per track | 2 | Go G1 / G2 / G3 · JVM J1 / J2 / J3 / J4 · pooler P1 (driver pool only) / P2 (PgBouncer) | G1 (+ G2 as alarm); J1 (J2 if the jOOQ licence is confirmed); P1 until load needs P2 | tier 2/3 + tier E (F207-F242); timing PENDING S28 | `cto`; backend track at `/setup-stack` |
| 3 | Edge proxy headers and client IP | 2 | X1 Caddy · X2 Traefik · X3 backend never trusts a tenant or forwarding header | either proxy **plus** X3; choice tied to the infra ADR | tier E (F262-F273) | `platform-lead` |
| 4 | Device enrolment and credential | 3 | D1-A..D1-D enrolment · D2-A mTLS · D2-B RFC 9421 signed requests · D2-C DPoP · D2-D bearer | D1-A + D2-B with a device-generated key, **conditional** on S31, S32 | tier 1 RFCs + tier E; S31/S32 PENDING → flagged | `cto` (credential ADR); founder for the OQ6 residue |
| 5 | Request scoping and revocation | 3 | D3-A ids only from the credential · D3-B · D3-C · D4-A / D4-B / D4-C | D3-A; D4-A (status read `FOR SHARE` per command; re-registration = new `device_id`) | tier E (F322-F329) | `cto` |
| 6 | Server sync state and restore | 3 | D5-A · D5-B · D5-C (control journal outside the restored database) | D5-C | F314 (tier 2), F330 (tier E); PITR S33 PENDING | `data-lead`, `sre-lead` |
| 7 | Branch scope and session | 4 | B1-A app only · B1-B RLS + app · B1-C · B1-D · B2-A opaque token · B2-B self-contained · B2-C | B1-B; B2-A | tier E (F417-F424); S42, S44 PENDING | `cto`; founder on roles per surface |
| 8 | Device store binding and re-registration | 4 | B4-A / B4-B / B4-C binding · B3-A / B3-B / B3-C / B3-D re-registration | B4-A + B4-B; B3-A (B3-B as exception) | S41, S43 PENDING → **flagged, not accepted** | key-custody ADR (`cto`, `security-lead`) |
| 9 | Export and deletion | 5 | DEL-1..DEL-4 · EXP-1..EXP-3 | DEL-2 (DEL-4 as a step, DEL-3 as escalation); EXP-2 | tier 1 law text (**unofficial English copies**) — every legal reading is a lead for counsel | counsel; `cto` (retention ADR) |

| 11 | Isolation test strategy | 6 | A generated matrix + story tests · B per-story only · C scanner/pen-test · D Android instrumented vs static · E DB catalogue + runtime-role tests | A + E (+ D-instrumented for AN-02/03/05/06); C deferred | tier 1 standards + tier E (F601-F624); S61-S63 PENDING | `qa-lead`, `security-lead` |

No option in any part is licence-blocked (hosted only); Redis 8.2 and Dragonfly would be blocked only in a customer bundle (part 5).

### 4.2 Cross-part consistency (merge observations — inference unless an F-number is cited)

1. **What a tenant is.** Part 1 leaves it open (its open question 2: venue or owner organisation). Part 4's tested model is tenant → branch → device with composite keys (F417-F422), which reads "tenant = the owner's store, branch = one venue"; the brief's platform line says store → branch → device (P3) while the price anchor is per venue and the value metric is open. Proposed reading for the founder to confirm: tenant = store (owner organisation), branch = venue, billing per branch (part-4 Q-OQ12). **Answered by the founder on 2026-10-17 — see § 5** (tenant = owner organisation, branch = venue, device = cashier PC; licence and price per cashier device, not per branch).
2. **One scope helper, set once, first.** Part 1 shows a second `set_config` silently switches tenant (F110); part 2 builds G1/J1 around exactly one call (F212, F239); part 4 sets a branch list once and must refuse a second call (F420); part 3 sets tenant, branch and device from the credential (F322, F324); part 5 adds a per-plan `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` (F517). These are one helper with one contract for setting names (part-2 open question "where `app.tenant_id` naming lives"), not four.
3. **One control journal, two uses.** Part 3's D5-C journal (enrolment, revocation, owner review decisions, security events) is the same store part 5 needs for deletion and erasure tombstones replayed after a restore (part-5 decision A.2). Its sink, retention and authority are one open question (P3-OQ6 with part-5 question 5).
4. **Re-registration agrees across parts 3 and 4** (new `device_id`, new store, crypto-erase only after drain or owner-acknowledged export). ETA receipt-chain continuity is open in both: P3-OQ7 and Q-OQ4 are merged into § 9.1 item 6.
5. **"A money fact is never lost" decides three questions together**: a revoked device's unsynced sales (P3-OQ1), uploads from a suspended tenant (part-5 decision A.6), and drain-before-reset (Q-OQ5). Founder rule OQ6 (sync note) is the shared principle.
6. **No cache for authorization state at Tier 1** is consistent across parts 3, 4, 5 and 6 (credential and device status, session scope, tenant status); only plan limits are cached (part 5).
7. **Transaction-local context is safe behind PgBouncer** in two independent runs (part-1 F115 on 1.26.0; part-2 F213a); session-level `SET` leaks (part-1 F115b; part-2 F210). If a pooler is adopted the floor is 1.26.1 (SCRAM impersonation fix, part-1 F114).
8. **Gaps one part left and another part closed.** Part-1 "ASVS V16 and 15.4.2" → part-6 F603; part-2 F291 → F604; part-3 F310 → F602 (no ASVS requirement states "identity from the credential, not the payload"; nearest items cited). Part 5 lists V11 and V16 as NOT SOURCEABLE (F527): part-4 F416 cites V11 (11.1.1, 11.1.2, 11.2.2, 11.3.3, 11.5.1) and part-6 F603 cites V16 from the official files. Part 6 lists the PG 18 `security_invoker` documentation as not fetched (F621): part-1 F107 sources it (tier 2). Still conflicting: ASVS 16.2.5 wording (part-6 F603 vs sync-note F436; use the file text) and MASTG-TEST-0216 vs MASWE-0004 numbering (part-6 F608, a conflict between OWASP's own artefacts).
9. **Target ASVS level is undecided** (part-6 Q1) while several mapped requirements are L3 (8.3.2, 8.3.3, 15.4.2, 1.2.10, 8.4.2). The level decides which of them are obligations.
10. **Not covered by any part**: how the platform operator (support, billing, N5 subscription control) reads across tenants without a login role that bypasses RLS (part-1 open question 7; ASVS 8.4.2 L3). It stays a named gap for the tenancy ADR.
11. **Part 6 did not read parts 4 and 5.** Its tests are written as outcomes and match part 4's B1-B design (DA-08: a manager of branch A1 is refused on A2; the owner sees both) and part 5's export rules (DA-06). The catalogue has **no test for part 5's "throttled, never dropped"** catch-up rule (429 + `Retry-After`, outbox kept, prefix acked); add one to SY at `/qa-plan`.
12. **Advisory searches are uneven**: OSV clean by exact version for pgx 5.11.0, Ktor 3.6.0, Exposed 1.5.1, Playwright 1.64.0, Vitest 5.0.3, React 19.3.0, Vite 8.3.4 (part-6 F616) and `golang.org/x/time` (part-5 F534); not searched: pgjdbc, HikariCP, jOOQ, Traefik 3.7.14, Redis 8.2, SQLite3MC, Testcontainers; GHSA mostly not run (part-2 F290, part 5, part 6).
13. **Crypto-erase is real only with a non-exportable key.** Part 4 finds SQLite pages and Windows shadow copies keep old data (F425, F426, F428), so erase rests on destroying the key; DPAPI user scope does not make that credible, a TPM-held key does (`[A]`, S41/S43 PENDING). Whether key destruction counts as erasure under the PDPL is for counsel (part-5 F536). This feeds the key-custody ADR together with desktop-note K1-K4.

Each part's option tables follow unchanged.

### Part 1 — PostgreSQL RLS re-verification at 18.6; tenancy model options

Four options for the tenancy model. All are PostgreSQL 18.6 on one hosted cluster unless stated; "per-tenant" costs use the measured numbers of F121/F122 (30 empty tables per tenant) and `[A]` extrapolations, so they scale the comparison and are not forecasts. Money: at 10,000 EGP/year per venue (F126) any per-tenant fixed cost in memory, connections or operator time counts against a small revenue line; its conversion to a currency figure is NOT SOURCEABLE.

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability (run, upgrade, self-hosted fit) |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|---------------------------------------------|
| A. `shared-schema-rls` | One database, one schema; every tenant table has `tenant_id`, ENABLE+FORCE RLS and a policy on a transaction-local setting (F101-F111); non-owner, non-bypass runtime roles; one pool set independent of tenant count. | Lowest marginal cost per tenant: ~0 MB beyond rows (F121). Needs the catalogue test, composite FKs, view and role rules (engineering cost up front). | Largest blast radius: one missing policy, one bypass-capable role, one session-level `SET` through a pooler (F115), one view owned by a bypass role (F108) or one single-column FK (F106) exposes **all** tenants at once. Engine bug class exists (CVE-2026-14666, fixed 18.6, F124). Cross-tenant covert channels via unique/FK (F105/F106) need schema discipline. Mitigated by tests, not by structure. | One policy predicate per query; tenant-leading indexes keep it an index condition (restaurant-menu/best-practices claim, not re-measured here). Catalogue stays small: 570 `pg_class` rows for 30 tables (F121). Noisy neighbours share everything (F120). | One schema, one migration (30 statements, 0.09 s, F122); one pool, one backup stream. Restore of one tenant alone is row-level surgery (see table below). PITR is cluster-wide (F118). Hosted only, so self-hosted fit is not a criterion. |
| B. `schema-per-tenant` | One database, a schema per tenant with identical tables; routing by `search_path` or qualified names; optionally a role per tenant (N roles, N pools) for a privilege boundary. | ~1.0 MB per tenant for 30 empty tables (F121); grows linearly. Tenant provisioning runs DDL for every table (0.113 s per tenant, F121). Role-per-tenant adds N logins and N pools. | Isolation rests on `search_path`/qualification and on grants; with one shared role the boundary is application code only (stated as `[A]`, no source). `search_path` handling behind a pooler has a documented history: PgBouncer only tracked it by default from 1.26.0 and says other bugs remain (F114); CVE-2025-12819 involved it. Migration fan-out is one logical change × N schemas; a partial failure leaves tenants on different versions (the "version dependencies" antipattern, F120). Azure names per-tenant tables in one database an antipattern at scale (F120, tier 4). | Catalogue grows with N × T: 15,415 `pg_class` and 81,161 `pg_attribute` rows at 100 tenants (F121); planner/relcache/autovacuum effects not measured (F129c, PENDING — S14). | Migrations: 3,000 statements in 0.21 s as one transaction (F122) but one failure rolls all back, lock footprint unmeasured. Per-tenant `pg_dump -n` 0.35 s and `pg_restore` into another database 0.25 s without touching others (F122, F116 caveat: subsets may not restore alone). PITR still cluster-wide (F118). |
| C. `db-per-tenant` | One cluster, a database per tenant (created from a template, F119), credentials/`CONNECT` per tenant; the application resolves tenant → database before connecting. | ~8.4 MB per empty tenant database (841 MB for 100, F121) plus a pooler partition per database × role (F113). Provisioning 0.102 s per database (F121) plus applying per-database settings and grants (not copied, F119). | Strongest structural boundary inside one cluster: a wrong query cannot cross databases (no cross-database references in a plain SQL session; stated as `[A]`, not tested here). Risks move to routing (wrong tenant → database mapping), credential count, migration fan-out (100 connections, 10.66 s, linear in N, F122; partial states) and connection exhaustion: with the default `max_connections` of about 100 (F123) and a pool per database and user (F113), N tenants × roles × pool size exceeds it quickly (`[A]`). Still one cluster: superuser, WAL and backups span all tenants (F118). | No policy predicate; each tenant's catalog is small (565 `pg_class` rows). Fresh connection to a tenant database needs its own pool slot; per-database autovacuum and cache effects not measured. | Easiest per-tenant logical restore or delete (`pg_dump`/`pg_restore` of one database 0.08 s for empty tables, `DROP DATABASE`), but needs automation for provisioning, migrations, routing, monitoring, per-tenant pool settings; a small team operates N+1 estates. Reporting across tenants requires fan-out queries (F120). |
| D. Hybrid: A by default, a dedicated database (C) for a tenant that needs it | Shared-schema RLS for ordinary venues; a per-tenant database only on a contractual, regulatory or noisy-neighbour trigger; a tenant-to-connection resolver port hides which. | Cost of A for most tenants; cost of C only where required; plus the resolver and a second migration target. | Two code paths to test; the dedicated path starts as rarely-exercised code. The resolver is itself a tenant-isolation component (wrong routing). | As A for most; as C for exceptions. | Two kinds of tenant to operate. Matches the mixed pattern Azure describes (F120, tier 4). |

Not considered further: a cluster or VM per tenant. It is the only way to get an independent PITR (F118) and is the most expensive per tenant by a wide margin; at 10,000 EGP/year per venue it is outside the anchor price. Cost figure: NOT SOURCEABLE (F129e).

Licence / bundle: PostgreSQL (all options) and PgBouncer (if used) touch none of L1-L4 or T1; hosted only, so no customer-bundle rule applies (PgBouncer's licence text not fetched, F129g). No option is licence-blocked.

#### Interaction with per-tenant export, deletion and restore of one tenant

| Concern | A. shared-schema-rls | B. schema-per-tenant | C. db-per-tenant | Basis |
|---------|----------------------|----------------------|------------------|-------|
| Export one tenant | `pg_dump --enable-row-security --inserts` as a non-bypass role with the tenant in `PGOPTIONS` returned only that tenant's rows in RLS tables (100 of 150) but the whole of any table without RLS (F117); alternative is per-table `COPY (SELECT ... WHERE tenant_id = $1)`. Output is INSERT text, not custom format. | `pg_dump -Fc -n <schema>`, 0.35 s (F122). | `pg_dump -Fc` of the database, 0.08 s (F122). | F116, F117, F122 |
| Delete one tenant | Row deletes across every table in foreign-key order, then vacuum; easy to miss a table without `tenant_id` (catalogue C3 shows them, F111). | `DROP SCHEMA ... CASCADE` `[A]` (not run). | `DROP DATABASE` `[A]` (not run). | F111 |
| Deletion versus backups | The WAL archive and base backups hold the tenant until they expire, in every model (cluster-wide, F118). Whether erasure must reach backups, and how that squares with ETA records kept at least 5 years (caller's constraint, F126), is a legal question: **needs counsel** (PDPL Law 151/2020 and ETA rules; this note cites no legal text). | same | same | F118, F126 |
| Restore one tenant alone | Restore the cluster to a side instance (PITR) or use a logical dump, then copy that tenant's rows back with conflict handling in key order; row-level surgery. | Drop the tenant schema and `pg_restore -n` from a logical dump (0.25 s into a separate database, F122); a subset dump may need dependencies (F116). | Drop and restore one database from its dump; other tenants untouched. | F118, F120, F122 |
| PITR of one tenant | Not possible without a whole-cluster restore into a side instance. | Same. | Same (one cluster). Only a cluster per tenant changes this. | F118 |
| Device side effects of restoring one tenant | A restored server state conflicts with sequence numbers devices already sent (sync-note F123/F124, F127); the same in all three. | same | same | F127 |

### Part 2 — Tenant context per backend track (pgx; pgjdbc, HikariCP, Exposed, jOOQ); edge proxy (Caddy, Traefik)

#### 4.1 Go track — how the tenant context is set (pgx 5.11.0)

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability (run, upgrade, hosted-only) |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|---------------------------------------------|
| G1 | One helper `WithTenant(ctx, pool, tenantID, fn)` built on `pgx.BeginTxFunc`; first statement `set_config('app.tenant_id', $1, true)` with a parsed UUID; repositories accept only the tx type that helper hands out (compile-time) | Low: about 20 lines plus a repo-wide rule | Remaining risk is a use case that bypasses the helper; RLS then returns 0 rows / 42501 (F207); second `set_config` call must be prevented in the helper (F212) | One extra statement per request; not measured. Works unchanged behind PgBouncer (F213) | Same code with or without a pooler; pooler needs `QueryExecModeExec` when `max_prepared_statements=0` (F213c/d) |
| G2 | G1 plus a `PrepareConn` guard that destroys any connection with a non-empty setting or open tx | Low-medium | Guard adds a failure mode (hook errors) and masks bugs rather than surfacing them unless it also logs/metrics | One more round trip per acquire (by construction; not measured) | Useful as an alarm: a guard hit means someone used `is_local=false`; metric it |
| G3 | Session-level `set_config(…, false)` at acquire plus `AfterRelease` / `PrepareConn` reset | Medium | **Unsafe behind a transaction-mode pooler** (F210: leaks across clients); correctness depends on hooks that cannot see the pooler's server connection | Saves nothing meaningful | Rejected |

#### 4.2 JVM track — how the tenant context is set

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| J1 | Exposed 1.5.1 `suspendTransaction` + a `JdbcTransaction.setTenant()` extension executing `set_config(?, ?, true)` as the first statement; tenant id passed as an explicit parameter or `asContextElement` | Low | Nested transaction blocks share the connection and can switch the outer context (F239/K5): forbid nested tenant blocks; HikariCP auto-commit trap if raw JDBC is used (F235a); `newSuspendedTransaction` is deprecated (F238) | One statement per transaction | HikariCP does not reset GUCs (F234), so `is_local=true` is mandatory; pgjdbc needs `prepareThreshold=0` behind a pooler with `max_prepared_statements=0` (F232) |
| J2 | jOOQ 3.21.9 `transactionResult` with `DSL.using(cfg)` for every statement | Medium (adds a dependency, codegen) | Using the outer `DSLContext` inside the callback bypasses the transaction (F242); PostgreSQL coverage of the free edition not sourced (F241) | Same | Licence confirmation needed before adoption |
| J3 | Raw JDBC through a project `DataSource` wrapper that begins a transaction, sets the context and gives the guard of F235d | Medium | More custom code to own | Guard adds a round trip per borrow | Only if neither Exposed nor jOOQ is chosen |
| J4 | Carrying the tenant id in a bare `ThreadLocal`/MDC | Lowest | **Leaks on reused threads and is wrong after suspension** (F240) | n/a | Rejected |

#### 4.3 Pooler

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| P1 | No external pooler: pgxpool / HikariCP only | None | Connection count limits at scale (not sized here) | Lowest latency | Simplest; default prepared statements work |
| P2 | PgBouncer in transaction mode in front of PG | Extra component to run and patch (stack reference notes its CVE history) | Session features unavailable (F215); prepared-statement mismatch errors if mis-configured (F213c, F232) | Not measured | Needs `max_prepared_statements` > 0 (pgx default mode OK) or `QueryExecModeExec` / `prepareThreshold=0`. Licence of PgBouncer: NOT SOURCEABLE (not read) |

#### 4.4 Edge proxy

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability / licence |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-----------------------|
| X1 | Caddy 2.11.7: `header_up -X-Tenant*`, `-Forwarded`, `-X-Real-IP`, explicit `X-Forwarded-Prefix/-Port/-Server` deletes, `header_up X-Client-IP {client_ip}`, global + site `log` filters | Low | Prefix over-match; logs still carry every inbound header unless deleted by name (F272) | Not measured | On the bundle allowlist; no L1–L4 conflict; hosted-only anyway. Underscore headers dropped by default (F264) |
| X2 | Traefik 3.7.14: headers middleware with an exact list of names, `forwardedHeaders` untouched for an edge deployment, access log with `drop` for path and IP and `headers.defaultMode: drop` | Low-medium | No wildcard strip (F269); underscore headers pass by default (set `aliasHeadersStrategy`); `X-Forwarded-Server` leaks hostname (F268); IP dropped, not masked (F271) | Not measured | On the bundle allowlist; no L1–L4 conflict. Docker-provider caveats in `docker-compose/best-practices.md` §9 apply |
| X3 | Either proxy plus a backend rule: never read a tenant from a header, ignore `X-Forwarded-*` except from the known proxy | Low | Needs a test per backend framework (Ktor/Netty underscore behaviour untested) | None | Defence in depth for X1/X2 |

No option here is licence-blocked under L1–L4/T1. PgBouncer, pgjdbc, HikariCP, Exposed licences were not read (NOT SOURCEABLE — turn budget).

### Part 3 — Device enrolment and credential; sync request scoping; server-side sync state

Licence check (L1-L4, T1): hosted only, so no customer bundle; none of the options needs Redis >= 7.4, Dragonfly in a bundle, a Mongo binary, or Grafana/Loki/Tempo/Mimir. **No option is licence-blocked.** Caddy and Traefik are the proxies already in the compose stack (stack reference); no library is chosen, so no library licence was read (NOT SOURCEABLE for `yaronf/httpsign` licence, not fetched). "Self-hosted fit" is therefore reported only as "n/a, hosted only".

#### 4.1 D1 — how a device is enrolled to one tenant and one branch

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability (run, upgrade, self-hosted fit) |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|---------------------------------------------|
| D1-A | **Owner-created, branch-bound, single-use code, then owner approval.** The owner (authenticated dashboard session) creates a code for one branch; the cashier PC redeems it with a freshly generated public key; the device becomes `pending`; the owner confirms in the dashboard by comparing a short key fingerprint shown on both screens; only then `active`. Shape follows Square's device code (F317), RFC 8628 (F306) and ASVS 6.4.1/6.5.x (F309). | Dashboard screens (create, list, approve), one unauthenticated redeem endpoint, a code table | An intercepted code is redeemed first by an attacker; the approval step catches it only if the owner really compares the fingerprint. Guessing is bounded by entropy, TTL and attempt cap (numbers open, P3-OQ4). | One-time, off the counter path | Branch is fixed by the authenticated owner, never by the device. Needs a rate-limited unauthenticated endpoint. |
| D1-B | **Device shows a code, owner types it in the dashboard** (the RFC 8628 direction, F306). The device starts the request; the owner binds it to a branch while approving. | Unauthenticated "create request" endpoint plus the same screens | Anyone on the internet can create pending requests (queue spam); an owner approves the wrong request unless the device identity is shown (RFC 8628 §5.4 warns of this). | One-time | No code travels to the device by hand; useful when the technician has no dashboard login. |
| D1-C | **Operator-provisioned credential in the installer** (server-issued secret or certificate baked into the build or a per-venue file). | Per-venue build or file handling | A copied installer is a copied identity; hard to make single-use; contradicts "device generates its own key". | none | Heavy for every new venue and replacement PC. |
| D1-D | **Cashier staff login as the binding.** | Low | A person is not a device: no per-device revocation, no clone signal; rejected for device binding (staff auth is another research topic). | none | n/a |

#### 4.2 D2 — the credential a sync request carries

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| D2-A | **mTLS client certificate** (private CA issues a leaf from the device's CSR; the proxy verifies; the backend reads the identity). Standard basis: RFC 8705 (F305), ASVS 12.1.3 / 12.1.4 / 12.3.5 (F308); Caddy `client_auth` with a `trust_pool` and optional `leaf` allow-list (F311). | A CA, issuance and renewal flow, proxy config; revocation is not built into Caddy (F311), so an application-side deny-list is needed anyway | Certificate expiry on a device that was offline for weeks locks it out (inference; ties to sync-note N5 grace); the hop that hands the certificate identity from proxy to backend is unsourced (F312); TPM-held keys as TLS client keys in each candidate runtime are untested (S32) | Handshake cost per connection, not per request | Extra moving part on the critical path; works only with a proxy that terminates TLS in front |
| D2-B | **Signed requests, RFC 9421**, with an ECDSA/Ed25519 key the device generated and the server holds as a public key; `keyid` is an opaque random id (F301). Cover method, authority, path, `Content-Digest`, `created`, `nonce`, `tag`; verify the digest (F302). Use a **server-supplied nonce** (`Accept-Signature`, F302) rather than device-clock freshness where the clock is unreliable. | Verifier code in Go or Kotlin; libraries exist for Go only as far as searched, one advisory on the main one (F332); the profile (which components, age limit, nonce rule) is ours to write (F302) | Spec leaves key resolution and freshness to us (F301, F302); a hand-rolled canonicalisation is a bug source; panic-on-crafted-header class of bug seen in the main Go library (F332) | One signature verify per request; unmeasured (S31) | No CA; revocation is a row update (F329); rotation is a staged key. Works behind any proxy that forwards headers and body unchanged (F302 notes intermediaries) |
| D2-C | **DPoP-bound short-lived access token** (RFC 9449). | Token endpoint, token store, refresh logic, plus the proof verifier (F304) | Mid-batch token expiry (sync-note abuse case 9); proof does not cover the body (F304) so the body still needs its own integrity check | Extra token round trip | Designed for browsers/AS-RS splits; most moving parts of the four |
| D2-D | **Bearer device token** (server-issued random secret, only its hash stored, held by the device under DPAPI K1/K2). | Lowest | Theft or a TLS-terminator log leak is full impersonation; no proof of possession; contradicts "device key never leaves the device" | Lowest | Easy rotation; weakest against disk clone (a clone has the same secret) |

Key generation is a sub-choice of D2-A/B: **device-generated key pair** (private key never leaves; server stores only a public key; matches the registered-certificate model of RFC 8705 §2.2, F305, and the non-exportable TPM property, F313/F318) versus **server-issued secret** (D2-D). Storage tiers are desktop-note K1-K3 (F318); TPM algorithm support is NOT SOURCEABLE (F313).

#### 4.3 D3 — what happens on a request that disagrees with the credential

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| D3-A | **Identity only from the credential; payload ids are claims; mismatch = refuse + event under the credential's tenant** (tested, F322, F326). Settings are set locally per transaction (F324). | One function, three settings, one event kind | An attacker with a valid key can fill the event table with mismatch events (flood); claimed ids are untrusted text (F322 note) | Negligible (one extra comparison) | Fails closed on missing context (F324) |
| D3-B | **Define no id fields in the request schema at all** and treat any extra field as a malformed request (400, no security event). | Contract change only | A client bug is not distinguished from an attack; no owner alert for a forged id | none | Simplest; cannot produce OQ6's "security event" for a forged id, so it does not satisfy the founder rule alone |
| D3-C | **Trust payload ids, validate against the credential in the application only**. | Low | One missed check is a cross-tenant write; rejected by ASVS 8.3.1 / 8.4.1 intent (F307) and by F326 (DB constraints exist to back the code) | none | n/a |

#### 4.4 D4 — revocation, rotation and re-registration

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| D4-A | **Status on the device row, read `FOR SHARE` by every command path; credential rotation by staged key; re-registration = new `device_id`.** Revocation orders against in-flight commands (F329 case 2). The old key signs the request that stages the new key (proof of possession); a lost-key replacement goes through D1-A again (ASVS 6.4.4, F309). | A row lock per command, a state machine, a partial unique index (one active credential per device, created in the test schema but not exercised) | Plain FK-only paths let an in-flight command commit after the revoke (F329 case 1); a restore resurrects a revoked device (F330) unless the runbook replays revocations (4.5) | A shared row lock per device command; devices are few | Revocation is immediate for new commands; lag only for the in-flight one |
| D4-B | **Revocation by short-lived credentials** (DPoP/mTLS cert lifetime of hours or days; no status check). | Renewal infrastructure | Offline devices expire (sync-note N5 grace conflicts); revocation lag = lifetime; ASVS 8.3.2 expects immediate effect or alert-and-revert (F307) | none | Heavy |
| D4-C | **Cached status** in the backend process (TTL of seconds). | Cache plus invalidation | Revocation lag up to the TTL; stale-after-restore (F330) | Saves one point read | Needs the cache decision of gate 5 |

#### 4.5 D5 — server-side sync state and what must survive a restore

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| D5-A | **All sync state in tenant-scoped tables of the main database, restore = whatever PITR yields.** Tested shapes in F322-F330. | Lowest | After a restore to an earlier point, revoked devices are active again, new devices are unknown, owner decisions and security events are gone (F330, F314) | Same DB | Simplest backup story; the restore runbook must say what to re-apply |
| D5-B | **Control tables (devices, credentials, revocations) in a second database with its own backup cadence.** | Second database, its own WAL archive | Composite foreign keys that F326 relies on cannot cross databases; two restore points to reconcile | Cross-database lookup per request | Doubles the backup surface |
| D5-C | **Main database plus an append-only control journal outside the restored database** (enrol, approve, revoke, rotate, owner review decisions, security events), written in the same transaction through an outbox and replayed idempotently by the restore runbook, with the watermark's authority being the backup catalogue. | Outbox, a durable sink (log pipeline or object store), a replay step in the runbook | Replay must be idempotent and ordered after the restore; journal retention must outlive the backup window (OQ29: 60 days device, server window shorter) | Small write amplification on rare events | Adds a runbook step and a drill (sre-lead owns restore drills, `coordination-rules.md`) |

#### 4.6 Table plan for area 2 (design proposal built on the tested schema; "survives" is a requirement, not a tested fact)

Scope key: T = tenant, B = branch, D = device. Actors are roles, not people; real role names belong to the roles part.

| Table | Scope | Writes | Reads | Must survive a server restore? |
|-------|-------|--------|-------|--------------------------------|
| `devices` (status `pending/active/retiring/revoked`, branch) | T, B, D | Owner dashboard (approve, revoke); the device only `last_seen_at` (F328) | Device (own row), dashboard, `auth_device()` | **Yes.** Status changes are server-originated and unrecoverable by device resend (F330) |
| `device_credentials` (opaque `key_id`, public key, state) | T, B, D | Enrolment service, dashboard (revoke) | Only `auth_device()` (F325) and the dashboard | **Yes** (else every device must re-enrol); revoked state must be re-applied |
| `enrolment_codes` (hash of code, branch, expiry, used_at, attempts) | T, B | Dashboard creates; redeem endpoint marks used | Redeem endpoint | No, but after any restore all pending codes should be expired by the runbook (a consumed code could reappear unconsumed inside its TTL: inference) |
| `sync_cursor`, `inbox` | T, D (B on inbox) | Sync apply path, device-context role | Dashboard (inbox, read) | Not as such: rebuilt from device resend (SC-19, sync-note) |
| `verified_restore_watermark` (hwm per device) | T, D (B via FK) | **Backup-verification job only**, a cross-tenant role with an explicit all-rows policy (F328) | Sync endpoint hands the device its own row; dashboard read-only | A rewound value is lower or equal, which is the safe direction for pruning (inference, PENDING - S33); it must be monotonic (trigger tested, F328) and its authority is the backup catalogue, not this table |
| `device_events` (`id_mismatch`, `revoked_credential_used`, `payload_mismatch`, `seq_conflict`, `server_behind_device`, `clone_suspected`, `restore_suspected`) | T, B, D | Sync apply path (insert-only grant); detection jobs | Dashboard (acknowledge column only); security staff via a platform path | **Yes for security evidence** (F330): events from before the failure are lost with the tail; `server_behind_device` is written after the restore so it persists |
| `platform_security_events` (no tenant: unknown key id, bad signature, malformed credential) | none | Authentication layer (platform role) | Operations only | Log/metric sink outside the database; needed because such a refusal has no tenant (F326) |
| `offline_periods` (device time and receipt time) | T, B, D | Sync apply path | Dashboard summary (SC-23) | Rebuilt only if the device keeps and resends its contact-gap record (sync-note TS-09); otherwise lost: P3-OQ9 |
| `review_queue` (reason, fact snapshot, status, decision) | T, B, D | Sync apply path inserts `open`; dashboard changes status columns only (F328) | Dashboard only; the device cannot read it (F328) | **Yes for decisions**: an owner "accepted" or a correction (a new record) is server-originated and lost on rewind (F330); `open` items are re-created by device resend |

### Part 4 — Branch scoping and session; encrypted local store binding and re-registration

Licence check: hosted only; nothing here needs Redis ≥ 7.4, Dragonfly in a bundle, a Mongo binary, or Grafana/Loki/Tempo/Mimir. SQLCipher Community Edition is BSD-style (desktop-note F103) and SQLite3MC is MIT (desktop-note F110); neither touches L1-L4/T1. **No option is licence-blocked.** Self-hosted fit: n/a (hosted only). Costs below are relative effort unless a number is cited; none is a forecast.

#### 4.1 B1 — where branch scope is enforced

| Option | Description | Cost | Risk | Performance | Operability |
|--------|-------------|------|------|-------------|-------------|
| B1-A | Application layer only: RLS isolates tenants (part-1), the service filters by branch in every query | Lowest up front | One forgotten filter exposes a sibling branch to a manager; the DB cannot catch it (ASVS 8.3.1 wants enforcement in a trusted layer, F307/part-3; this is the "data-specific access" risk 8.2.2) | Predicate in each query, no per-row policy cost | Reports are easy; every new query needs review |
| B1-B | RLS tenant policy (permissive) plus a **RESTRICTIVE branch policy** on a list-valued transaction-local setting (`app.branch_ids`) or `app.branch_all='on'`, set once per transaction by the same helper that sets the tenant, **and** application-layer role/function checks (API5, F412) | Medium: one more setting, one more policy per branch-scoped table, composite FKs with `branch_id` (F418) | Second `SET LOCAL` widens scope silently (F420) so the helper must refuse it; a wrong list from the caller is a wrong scope, so the lookup is the new trust point; materialized views and view owners bypass it (F423, part-1 F108) | Branch predicate stayed a Filter after a tenant/time index scan: 3.0-3.5 ms vs 0.7 ms for the owner on 15,000 rows, one warm run (F424) | Same pooler rules as the tenant setting (part-1 F115); catalogue test must also check the branch policy exists |
