- **Legal**: none cited; PDPL points stay with `privacy-compliance-specialist` (needs counsel).

## 7. Performance & Caching Notes

- **Expected load**: no sourced figures in any part (commands per branch per day, tenant count, data volume: NOT SOURCEABLE); `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist, so every budget is "not checked", never "met". The founder's 3 s counter ceiling binds the cashier device, not these server paths.
- **Hot paths**: per-request credential or session resolution before any tenant context (parts 3, 4); one extra scope statement per transaction (parts 1, 2, 4, 5); the `FOR SHARE` device read on each sync command (part 3); the branch predicate on dashboard lists and reports (part-4 F424: a row filter, not an index condition, in the small test); the limiter check (part 5).
- **Caching decision (gate 5)**: no cache for tenant context, credential and device status, session scope or tenant status at Tier 1 (reason: revocation and assignment changes must take effect at once, ASVS 8.3.2); plan limits are cached (cache-aside, `limits:{tenant}`, short configurable TTL, invalidate on change, single-flight). Any cache key carries the tenant; never a client-supplied tenant or branch.
- **Budgets affected**: sync and dashboard p95, queries per request (+1 lookup, +1 scope statement), payload and header size caps. Measurements PENDING: S14, S28, S34, S42, S50, S51.

### Part 1 — PostgreSQL RLS re-verification at 18.6; tenancy model options

- **Expected load**: 3 venues, one cashier device each, a command stream into the server inbox plus owner dashboard reads (F126, F127); no sourced request rates, row counts or data volumes exist — NOT SOURCEABLE; do not invent. `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist (`docs/architecture/` is absent), so every budget check is "not checked", never "met". The product's 3 s counter ceiling is a client-local budget and does not constrain the server model.
- **Hot paths**: device-to-tenant resolution on every sync request (a lookup before any tenant context exists); the inbox apply path (sync-note F124/F126); the policy predicate on each tenant query (A) or the routing lookup (C, D). Index every tenant table with `tenant_id` leading (migrations rule).
- **Measured (S11, empty tables, single run)**: see F121/F122. Not measured: concurrency, large N, real data, planner or catalog-cache effects (PENDING — S14).
- **Caching candidates** (decision goes to the spec or `cache-design.md`): tenant status (active/suspended, N5) and device-to-tenant resolution; any key carries the tenant id (`caching-standard.md`); authorization-relevant state must not outlive the accepted revocation latency; under Option C or D the tenant-to-database routing entry is cacheable with invalidation on tenant move. No cache decision is made here.
- **Budgets affected**: none can be named until `perf-budgets.md` exists; the extra per-transaction `set_config` statement (A) is one round trip, not measured in a Go driver here.

### Part 2 — Tenant context per backend track (pgx; pgjdbc, HikariCP, Exposed, jOOQ); edge proxy (Caddy, Traefik)

- **Expected load**: not available (no spec, no `perf-budgets.md` read in this part). The memory-recorded constraint is a 3 s worst-case ceiling for counter actions on a 4 GB PC; that concerns the cashier client, not this server path.
- **Hot paths**: every authenticated request becomes one transaction with one extra `set_config` statement. Timing of that statement, of the `PrepareConn`/`DataSource` guard and of PgBouncer was not measured — NOT SOURCEABLE — turn budget. A load test on a representative endpoint is a Gate 6 task.
- **Caching candidates**: none for the tenant context (per-transaction, cannot be cached by design). Proxy-level caching not considered. The `caching-standard.md` decision for the stories that use this: "no cache for tenant context; reason: transaction-local setting".
- **Budgets affected**: p95 latency and queries per request in `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` (+1 statement per request); the file was not read here.
- Pool sizing/acquire timeout: a leaked open transaction starves a pool (F214); set an acquire timeout and alert on saturation.

### Part 3 — Device enrolment and credential; sync request scoping; server-side sync state

- **Expected load:** one device per branch in Tier 1, so a handful to a few hundred devices; per-device sync is background traffic (counter actions are not allowed to wait on it: counter ceiling 3 s per the project memory, numbers in `perf-budgets.md` after spike phase 3, which does not exist yet — `docs/architecture/` is absent). No request-rate number is sourced; NOT SOURCEABLE — no product volume figure in the brief was read for this part.
- **Hot paths:** (1) per-request credential resolution (`auth_device(key_id)`: one point read by primary key plus one join to the device row); (2) the command path taking a shared row lock on the device row (F329) and the cursor row `FOR UPDATE` (sync-note F125/F126), which already serialises a device's commands, so the extra lock adds no new contention between different devices; (3) signature verification and `Content-Digest` hashing over batch bodies (cost unmeasured: PENDING - S31); (4) the dashboard review queue and events lists (index leading with `tenant_id`, then `status`/`created_at`; needs paging).
- **Caching decision (gate 5), recommended:** **no cache for credential or device status at Tier 1.** Reason: the lookup is a primary-key read on a tiny table; a cache adds revocation lag that ASVS 8.3.2 (F307) asks to avoid, and a cached "active" outlives a restore that changed the truth (F330). Revisit only if S34 shows the lookup above its share of the sync budget; then a per-process cache with a TTL from configuration and explicit invalidation on revoke. Anything cached must be keyed by `key_id`, never by a tenant taken from the payload. Enrolment-code and replay-nonce stores need a short-TTL store: Postgres rows with an expiry column are enough at this volume; Valkey (L1) is not needed and nothing here touches Dragonfly (L2).
- **Budgets affected:** `perf-budgets.md` is absent; queries per request for the sync endpoint should be stated there (this part adds at most 1 lookup plus 1 lock read per request: inference). Payload and header size caps for the verifier (F332, F302) go into the same budget.

### Part 4 — Branch scoping and session; encrypted local store binding and re-registration

- **Expected load**: three venues today, one device per branch, a chain of 2+ branches per owner; no sourced request rates or row counts (NOT SOURCEABLE). `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist: every budget check is "not checked". The 3 s counter ceiling concerns the cashier device; re-registration and erase are off the counter path.
- **Hot paths**: (1) per-request session → user → role → branch list lookup (B2-A) before any tenant context; (2) the branch predicate on list/report queries: in the test it stayed a row filter after a tenant+time index scan (F424), so a branch with few rows in a large tenant is read in full for the tenant range; indexing `(tenant_id, branch_id, created_at)` did not become an index condition on `branch_id` here (the plan showed 4 index searches on the other two columns); (3) owner cross-branch aggregates over all branches.
- **Caching candidates** (decision goes to the spec / `cache-design.md`; gate 5): session/scope lookup. Recommended: **no cache at Tier 1**; the lookup is a primary-key read, a cache would add the revocation and assignment-change lag that B2-A exists to avoid (F421, F410 7.4.1) and ASVS 8.3.2 asks for immediate effect (part-3 F307). If load requires one: per-process, TTL from configuration, key = session id (never a client-supplied tenant/branch), explicit invalidation on role/assignment/branch change and on logout. Report rollups: tenant and branch in every key.
- **Budgets affected**: queries per request (+1 session/scope lookup, +1 `set_config`), p95 of dashboard list endpoints, report latency. S42 (hoisted lookup) and a larger-table run are PENDING.

### Part 5 — Tenant export and deletion (PDPL, ETA retention); noisy-neighbour limits on sync

- **Expected load:** no sourced figures (commands per branch per day, command size, tenant count: NOT SOURCEABLE, sync-note F131); `docs/architecture/perf-budgets.md` does not exist, so every budget is "not checked". Three venues, one device each today.
- **Hot paths:** the limiter check on every sync request (a verified-credential lookup is already needed, part-3); the per-transaction `SET LOCAL` of the plan timeout next to the tenant context (part-1/2); export streaming over a large archive; inbox apply worker fairness. None measured.
- **Caching decision (gate 5), for the stories that use this:** plan limits and tenant overrides — cache-aside, key `limits:{tenant}`, TTL short and configurable, invalidation on plan or override change, stampede protection by single-flight; tenant status (active/suspended/closed) is **not** cached at Tier 1 for revocation reasons (part-3 § 7), so the limiter reads status from the same lookup that resolves the device. Limiter counters are not a cache; they are state with TTL. No cache for the export (one-off).
- **Budgets affected:** p95 latency of the sync endpoint (+1 limiter round trip when RL-C), queries per request (+0 if limits are cached), payload size caps (batch bytes). Export must not run on the primary during counter hours `[A]`.
- **Measure before choosing numbers:** PENDING — S50 (`SET LOCAL statement_timeout` behind a transaction-mode pooler), S51 (Lua token bucket/GCRA on Valkey 9.1.2: atomicity, latency, `EVAL` ACL), S52 (per-tenant `COPY` export as the runtime role: RLS filtered, table coverage). None run: fetch and turn budget.

### Part 6 — Cross-tenant negative test catalogue; ASVS / MASVS / API Top 10 mapping

- **Expected load**: test-time only. The matrix size is objects × routes × principals (not yet countable: no contract exists). Each Postgres container start dominated S60's runtime (a few seconds; exact time not recorded).
- **Hot paths affected by the tests**: the credential lookup and `FOR SHARE` device read on every sync command (SY-07, SY-15) and the per-request tenant setting; the tests check correctness, not latency. The counter 3 s ceiling and `perf-budgets.md` (absent; written after spike phase 3) are untouched: no budget is claimed here.
- **Caching candidates**: none introduced. Tests DA-15, DA-16 and AN-02/03 verify that any future cache (HTTP, application, SPA query cache, app memory) is keyed by tenant and cleared on logout (ASVS 14.2.2, 14.3.1). Part-3's recommendation is no cache for credential or device status at Tier 1; SY-07 fails if one is added without invalidation. Gate 5 decision for this note: no cache; reason above.
- **Budgets affected**: none measured. PENDING — S61 (pooled-connection test) also gives the first cost of resetting context per checkout.

## 8. Observability Notes

Per `observability-standard.md`: low-cardinality span and metric names; `tenant_id`, `branch_id`, `device_id` as span attributes and log fields only, never metric labels (except the tenant while the tenant count is bounded); `trace_id` in logs; no PII, no key material, claimed ids truncated and escaped.

- **Spans** (merged, names are proposals): `db.tenant_tx`, `auth.session.resolve`, `sync.auth.verify`, `sync.auth.resolve_device`, `sync.apply`, `sync.limit.check`, `device.enrol.*`, `device.revoke`, `device.reregister` (`store.drain_check`, `store.export`, `store.crypto_erase`), `restore.watermark.update`, `tenant.export`, `tenant.closure.step`.
- **Metrics**: `tenant_context_missing_total`, `tenant_context_switch_refused_total`, `branch_scope_switch_refused_total`, `authz_denied_total{layer}`, `sync_auth_total{outcome}`, `sync.refused{reason}`, `review_queue_open`, `restore_watermark_lag_seconds`, `control_journal_lag_seconds`, `ratelimit_decisions_total`, `ratelimit_fail_open_total`, pool wait and saturation.
- **Log events**: `tenant_context_missing`, `branch_scope.refused`, `sync.refused`, `device.revoked`, `store.binding_mismatch`, `device.reregistration.*`, `rate_limited`, `tenant_export_*`, `tenant_closure_*`, `breach_suspected`, `restore.replay.*`.
- **Alerts / runbooks**: page on any missing or switched scope in production; owner alert on a forged or unbound command (founder rule OQ6), aggregated and rate-limited; runbooks for restore with control-journal replay, replacing or moving a cashier PC, tenant closure, PDPL breach (72 hours to the Centre / three working days to subjects, part-5 F509) and subject requests (six working days, part-5 F506); "isolation test failed in CI" treated as S1 until classified. Owners `observability-engineer`, `sre-lead`, `qa-lead`.

### Part 1 — PostgreSQL RLS re-verification at 18.6; tenancy model options

Per `observability-standard.md` (span names low-cardinality; `app.tenant_id` as a span attribute and a log field; no high-cardinality metric labels; `tenant_id` as a metric label only for bounded tenant counts; no PII):
- **Spans**: a `db.tenant_tx` span around each tenant transaction with `app.tenant_id`; a child span for the tenant-context statement is not needed as a separate business span; a span for device-to-tenant resolution; under B or C, a span for the tenant-to-schema/database routing step.
- **Metrics (RED / business)**: `tenant_context_missing_total`, `tenant_context_switch_refused_total` (a second set in one transaction, F110), `db_pool_wait_seconds` and `db_connections_in_use` per pool, `db_lock_timeout_total`; for B/C also `migration_tenants_pending` and `migration_duration_seconds` and a per-tenant backup-age gauge only if the tenant count is bounded (the standard allows tenant labels only then). Pooler statistics: NOT SOURCEABLE (PgBouncer admin console not researched).
- **Log events** (no PII, no secrets, `trace_id`): `tenant_context_missing`, `tenant_context_switch_refused`, `tenant_id_malformed` (SQLSTATE 22P02, F110), `db_role_misconfigured` (startup self-check of the role attributes, C4-style, F111), `tenant_moved` (D). Catalogue-test failures are CI output, not runtime logs.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: page on any `tenant_context_missing` or `tenant_context_switch_refused` in production; runbooks for restoring one tenant (per option, F118) and for role bootstrap; an SLO for sync-inbox apply latency is owned by the sync part.

### Part 2 — Tenant context per backend track (pgx; pgjdbc, HikariCP, Exposed, jOOQ); edge proxy (Caddy, Traefik)

(Names are proposals; `observability-standard.md` was not re-read in this part.)
- **Spans**: one DB span per request transaction; span event `tenant_context.set` (attributes: none of tenant name or user data; the tenant id only if the standard allows internal ids in traces); span on connection acquire so starvation (F214) shows as wait time.
- **Metrics (RED / business)**: pool acquire wait and saturation (pgxpool stat / Hikari metrics); counter `tenant_context_guard_destroyed_total` for the `PrepareConn` / `DataSource` guard (G2/F235d); counter of RLS violations (SQLSTATE 42501) and of 22P02 from the tenant setting; proxy request rate/latency by status. Do not label metrics with tenant id (cardinality; reasoning, not a sourced claim).
- **Log events**: `tenant_context_missing` (helper bypass detected), `tenant_context_guard_hit`, `db_rls_violation` (SQLSTATE only, no row data), all carrying `trace_id`; proxy access log with path and IP dropped or redacted (F271, F272); no spoofed header values in logs (delete by name in Caddy; `headers.defaultMode: drop` in Traefik).
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook**: alert on any guard hit or 42501 in production; runbook entry "connection pool starvation from a leaked transaction". Owner `observability-engineer`.

### Part 3 — Device enrolment and credential; sync request scoping; server-side sync state

Names are proposals for `observability-engineer`; label values stay low-cardinality (no `device_id`, `key_id`, code or claimed ids as metric labels; tenant and device ids only as span attributes and log fields, subject to the "no PII" rule).

- **Spans:** `sync.auth.verify` (signature, digest, nonce; attribute `outcome`), `sync.auth.resolve_device` (the `auth_device` lookup), `sync.apply` (child of the request, attributes `tenant_id`, `branch_id`, `device_id`, `outcome`), `device.enrol.create_code`, `device.enrol.redeem`, `device.enrol.approve`, `device.revoke`, `device.credential.rotate`, `restore.watermark.update` (job).
- **Metrics (RED / business):** `sync_auth_total{outcome=ok|unknown_key|bad_signature|bad_digest|replay|stale|revoked|id_mismatch}` and duration histogram; `device_enrolment_total{step=created|redeemed|approved|expired|rejected}`; `device_revocation_total{reason}`; `sync_event_total{kind=…}` (the `device_events` kinds); `review_queue_open` gauge and `review_queue_age_seconds` histogram; `restore_watermark_lag_seconds` (age of the newest verified watermark); `control_journal_lag_seconds` (D5-C).
- **Log events (structured, carry `trace_id`; no secrets, no code values, no key material, claimed ids truncated and escaped):** `device.enrol.code_created`, `.code_redeemed`, `.code_failed` (with attempt count only), `.approved`, `device.revoked`, `device.credential.staged|activated|retired`, `sync.refused` with `reason` and the credential-derived ids, `sync.event.recorded` with `kind`, `review.queue.opened|decided`, `restore.watermark.updated`, `restore.replay.started|finished` (journal replay). ASVS 16.2.5 and 16.3.3 (sync-note F436) apply.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed:** owner alert on a forged or unbound command (founder rule OQ6), aggregated and rate-limited (abuse case 2-3); platform alert on spikes of `unknown_key` or `bad_signature`; alert when the review queue has items older than a configured age; alert when the control journal lags; a **restore runbook** that includes expiring pending enrolment codes, replaying the control journal, re-checking every device status against the journal, and recomputing watermarks — `sre-lead` owns the drill. Runbook and SLO files do not exist yet.

### Part 4 — Branch scoping and session; encrypted local store binding and re-registration

Per `observability-standard.md`: `tenant_id`/`branch_id` as span attributes and log fields only, not metric labels (unbounded or high-cardinality), no PII, no key material.
- **Spans**: `auth.session.resolve` (outcome), `db.tenant_tx` with `app.tenant_id` and branch-scope kind (`all|list`, count only), `report.branch_aggregate`; on the device: `store.open` (outcome `ok|binding_mismatch|wrong_key|missing`), `device.reregister` with children `store.drain_check`, `store.export`, `store.crypto_erase`.
- **Metrics**: `branch_scope_set_total{kind=all|list}`, `branch_scope_missing_total`, `branch_scope_switch_refused_total` (second set, F420), `authz_denied_total{layer=app|rls,reason}`, `session_invalidated_total{reason=logout|role_change|assignment_change|disabled}`, `store_open_total{outcome}`, `device_reregistration_total{outcome=drained|exported|blocked}`.
- **Log events** (structured, `trace_id`, no PII, no secrets): `branch_scope.refused` (client-named branch outside the derived set), `branch_scope.switch_refused`, `staff.assignment_changed` (audit, P6), `store.binding_mismatch`, `store.open_failed`, `device.reregistration.started|drain_blocked|export_written|key_destroyed|store_erased`, `device.reset_notified_owner`.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook**: page on any `branch_scope_missing` or `branch_scope_switch_refused` in production; owner notification on `device.reset`; a runbook "replace or move a cashier PC" (owner approval, drain, export, erase) owned by `sre-lead`/`support`; nothing exists yet.

### Part 5 — Tenant export and deletion (PDPL, ETA retention); noisy-neighbour limits on sync

(Per `observability-standard.md`: low-cardinality names, `trace_id` on logs, no PII; the standard was not re-read in this part, so names are proposals.)
- **Spans:** `sync.limit.check` (attributes: limit key, decision), `tenant.export` with child spans per table, `tenant.closure.step` per state, `db.tenant_tx` unchanged (part-1).
- **Metrics:** `ratelimit_decisions_total{route,limit,decision}`; `sync_throttled_total`; `sync_retry_after_seconds` (histogram); `ratelimit_store_errors_total` and `ratelimit_fail_open_total`; `sync_batch_commands` and `sync_batch_bytes` histograms; `sync_concurrent_syncs` gauge; `tenant_export_duration_seconds`, `tenant_export_bytes`; `tenant_closure_step_total{step}`; `tenant_archive_retain_until_days` (bounded); `backup_oldest_age_days` (vs the 60-day rule). Tenant id as a label only while the tenant count is bounded (part-1 § 8).
- **Log events:** `rate_limited` (tenant id, device id as internal ids, limit key, retry-after), `limit_override_applied`, `tenant_export_requested/completed/failed`, `tenant_closure_requested/step_done/completed` with date and time (the processor record of F508), `tenant_suspended_sync_accepted`, `breach_suspected` (starts the 72-hour clock, F509). No names, phone numbers, passcodes or payloads.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook:** alert on persistent throttling of one tenant, on limiter fail-open, on a closure step stuck, on any export of unusual size; runbooks for tenant closure, restore-with-tombstones, PDPL breach (72 h / 3 business days) and subject requests (six working days, F506).

### Part 6 — Cross-tenant negative test catalogue; ASVS / MASVS / API Top 10 mapping

- **Spans** the tests assert exist: one server span per sync and dashboard request carrying `tenant_id` (resolved, never claimed) and `trace_id`; a span event or child span for the refusal reason on SY-01..04; job spans carry the tenant they ran under (DA-18).
- **Metrics**: `sync.refused` counter by `reason` (`id_mismatch`, `unknown_credential`, `revoked`, `payload_mismatch`, `seq_conflict`), authorization-denied counter on the dashboard API by route template (never raw id), RED metrics per endpoint; alert when refusals for one tenant spike (rate-limit/aggregation is another part's).
- **Log events** (structured, `trace_id`, no PII, no secrets, claimed ids truncated and escaped, ASVS 16.2.1, 16.2.5, 16.3.2, 16.3.3, 16.4.1): `sync.refused`, `device.revoked`, `authz.denied` (object type, route template, principal id, no foreign object content), `export.requested|downloaded|denied`, `session.revoked`. Tests SY-16 and AN-08 enforce the no-leak side.
- **Alerts / SLO / runbook needed**: owner alert on forged-id refusal (founder OQ6); runbook for "isolation test failed in CI" (stop merge, treat as S1 until classified) — owner `qa-lead` / `sre-lead`.

Merged 2026-10-17 by `/research` with the Write tool (a script merge was denied). Each part's sections 1-9 are copied unchanged under a "Part n" heading with their own sub-headings demoted one level; part 2's extra sections (§ 10 proposed pins, evidence index) sit under its § 9 block. Written by the merge: the header, § 1 lead, § 2 baseline table, § 3 lead, §§ 4.1-4.2, the § 5 lead, the §§ 6-8 leads, §§ 9.1-9.4. No finding text was changed. Part-6 Q10 is answered by § 4.2 item 11 (the catalogue matches parts 4 and 5; one test added to the backlog); part-6 Q9's `security_invoker` item by part-1 F107 (§ 4.2 item 8). The part files `docs/research/tenant-isolation.part-1.md` … `part-6.md` are kept until the user approves this note, then removed.
